[36muser[0m
Assess this manuscript's publication potential honestly. You are not its author and you gain nothing from flattering it.

Read main.tex and its section files, the artifacts/ verification scripts and their output, and any oracle_sprint_*.md replies in artifacts/ (those are an external expert's rounds, already filtered by a verifier).

Answer these, in order, in ENGLISH, concisely:

1. HEADLINE RESULT. State the single strongest theorem in one sentence, as a mathematician would state it. Give its label.

2. IS IT ACTUALLY NEW? Judge independently. Search arXiv/MathSciNet-style knowledge for the nearest prior work and name it. Then classify the headline as one of:
   - GENUINELY NEW (no prior work does this)
   - INCREMENTAL (a known result extended by a step)
   - REPACKAGING (known content in new notation)
   Justify in two sentences with the specific prior work.

3. DEPTH OF THE MATHEMATICS. Is the proof technique substantial (new mechanism, hard estimate, genuine obstruction overcome), or is it bookkeeping/computation? Be blunt.

4. TOP-JOURNAL VERDICT. Could this, as it stands, plausibly be accepted at a top-tier journal in its field (Annals/Inventiones/JAMS tier = TIER-1; JFA/ETDS/Trans.AMS/Math.Comp tier = TIER-2; solid specialist journal = TIER-3; borderline/niche = TIER-4)? Give ONE tier and one sentence of justification. Do not inflate.

5. WHAT WOULD MOVE IT UP ONE TIER. The single most valuable missing theorem or strengthening, stated precisely. If nothing realistic would move it up, say so.

6. WEAKEST LINK. The claim most likely to draw a referee's objection, and why.

Be concise: at most 30 lines total. No praise, no hedging filler.

NOTE: this manuscript was revised recently. Judge the CURRENT text only. Do not assume earlier assessments were right, and do not look for reasons to agree with them.

## Instruction Priority

Superpowers skills override default system prompt behavior, but **user instructions always take precedence**:

1. **User's explicit instructions** (CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, AGENTS.md, direct requests) â€” highest priority
2. **Superpowers skills** â€” override default system behavior where they conflict
3. **Default system prompt** â€” lowest priority

If CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, or AGENTS.md says "don't use TDD" and a skill says "always use TDD," follow the user's instructions. The user is in control.

## How to Access Skills

**In Claude Code:** Use the `Skill` tool. When you invoke a skill, its content is loaded and presented to youâ€”follow it directly. Never use the Read tool on skill files.

**In Copilot CLI:** Use the `skill` tool. Skills are auto-discovered from installed plugins. The `skill` tool works the same as Claude Code's `Skill` tool.

**In Gemini CLI:** Skills activate via the `activate_skill` tool. Gemini loads skill metadata at session start and activates the full content on demand.

**In other environments:** Check your platform's documentation for how skills are loaded.

## Platform Adaptation

Skills use Claude Code tool names. Non-CC platforms: see `references/copilot-tools.md` (Copilot CLI), `references/codex-tools.md` (Codex) for tool equivalents. Gemini CLI users get the tool mapping loaded automatically via GEMINI.md.

# Using Skills

## The Rule

**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action.** Even a 1% chance a skill might apply means that you should invoke the skill to check. If an invoked skill turns out to be wrong for the situation, you don't need to use it.

```dot
digraph skill_flow {
    "User message received" [shape=doublecircle];
    "About to EnterPlanMode?" [shape=doublecircle];
    "Already brainstormed?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke brainstorming skill" [shape=box];
    "Might any skill apply?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke Skill tool" [shape=box];
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" [shape=box];
    "Has checklist?" [shape=diamond];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [shape=box];
    "Follow skill exactly" [shape=box];
    "Respond (including clarifications)" [shape=doublecircle];

    "About to EnterPlanMode?" -> "Already brainstormed?";
    "Already brainstormed?" -> "Invoke brainstorming skill" [label="no"];
    "Already brainstormed?" -> "Might any skill apply?" [label="yes"];
    "Invoke brainstorming skill" -> "Might any skill apply?";

    "User message received" -> "Might any skill apply?";
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Invoke Skill tool" [label="yes, even 1%"];
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Respond (including clarifications)" [label="definitely not"];
    "Invoke Skill tool" -> "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'";
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" -> "Has checklist?";
    "Has checklist?" -> "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [label="yes"];
    "Has checklist?" -> "Follow skill exactly" [label="no"];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" -> "Follow skill exactly";
}
```

## Red Flags

These thoughts mean STOPâ€”you're rationalizing:

| Thought | Reality |
|---------|---------|
| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept â‰  using the skill. Invoke it. |

## Skill Priority

When multiple skills could apply, use this order:

1. **Process skills first** (brainstorming, debugging) - these determine HOW to approach the task
2. **Implementation skills second** (frontend-design, mcp-builder) - these guide execution

"Let's build X" â†’ brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
"Fix this bug" â†’ debugging first, then domain-specific skills.

## Skill Types

**Rigid** (TDD, debugging): Follow exactly. Don't adapt away discipline.

**Flexible** (patterns): Adapt principles to context.

The skill itself tells you which.

## User Instructions

Instructions say WHAT, not HOW. "Add X" or "Fix Y" doesn't mean skip workflows.

\title[Adams-corrected Frobenius products]{Adams-corrected Frobenius-class product constants for finite-group extensions of one-sided shifts of finite type}

\author[Haobo Ma]{Haobo Ma}
\address{AELF PTE. LTD., \#14-02, Marina Bay Financial Centre Tower 1,
8 Marina Boulevard, Singapore 018981, Singapore}
\email{auric@aelf.io}
\author[Wenlin Zhang]{Wenlin Zhang}
\address{National University of Singapore, 21 Lower Kent Ridge Road,
Singapore 119077, Singapore}
\email{e1327962@u.nus.edu}
\thanks{Corresponding author: Wenlin Zhang.}
\date{}
\renewcommand{\shortauthors}{H. Ma and W. Zhang}

\begin{document}

\begin{abstract}
Let \(X_\Gamma^+\) be a mixing one-sided edge shift, let \(G\) be finite,
and let a one-step cocycle label each primitive orbit \(\gamma\) by a
conjugacy class \([g_\gamma]\).  We determine the constant in the
Frobenius-class Mertens product by separating two logarithms that coincide in
the scalar theory but not for general finite groups.  The periodic Artin
logarithm repeats both the orbit and its label,
\[
 L_\rho(z)=\sum_{r\geq1}\Pi_{\psi^r\chi_\rho}(z^r)/r,
 \qquad (\psi^r\chi_\rho)(g)=\chi_\rho(g^r),
\]
whereas the class product repeats the scalar weight while keeping the
primitive label fixed.  Its coordinate is therefore
\(F_\rho(z)=\sum_{r\geq1}\Pi_{\chi_\rho}(z^r)/r\).  Under the strict twisted
spectral gap, we prove an Adams--M\"obius determinant formula for
\(F_\rho(\lambda^{-1})\) and hence for every class-product constant.

This corrects two independent defects in the theorem of Mohamed and Noorani
(1999).  First, mixing of the base alone does not imply Frobenius
equidistribution: an extension-level aperiodicity condition, imposed here as
the strict twisted gap, is also necessary.  Second, their constant replaces
\(\chi_\rho(g_\gamma)\) by \(\chi_\rho(g_\gamma^r)\) in the \(r\)-th
logarithmic term.  A trivial \(C_2\)-cocycle disproves the exponent under base
mixing alone, while a strict-gap \(S_3\) extension disproves the asserted
Artin coordinate.  Under the stated gap hypothesis the exponent is
\(|C|/|G|\), and the non-trivial constant coordinates are the fixed-label
values above.  We further identify the exact positive quotient-cover factor
missed by the Artin substitution, proving that it strictly underestimates the
identity-class constant in every non-trivial strict-gap extension.  Harmonic
renormalization gives an exponentially accurate product asymptotic for every
union of Frobenius classes.  Finally, an exact four-vertex \(C_2\) pair shows
that all class constants together do not determine the primitive class
profile, even under strict gap.  The rational Mahler certificate makes the
remaining binary inverse problem effective: for a \(v\)-vertex same-base
pair, fewer than \(16v^3\) prescribed algebraic radial samples recover the
entire primitive length--class profile.  For general finite groups, an
analytic radial profile with an interior accumulation set still gives
recovery.
\end{abstract}

\section*{Guide to the references}
The classical orbit-counting and product scale is taken from
\cite{BowenLanford1970Zeta,Parry1983PrimeOrbit,ParryPollicott1990Zeta,
ParryPollicott1986Chebotarev,NooraniParry1992ChebotarevShifts,
Sharp1991Mertens,HasegawaSaito2016GraphMertens}.  Necklace/Witt inversion is
taken from
\cite{MetropolisRota1983Necklaces}.  The Artin-function antecedent and the
theorem corrected here are
\cite{Noorani1995ChebotarevFiniteExtensions,
MeslizaNoorani1999FrobeniusMertens}; a post-1999 use is discussed through
\cite{NordinNooraniMohd2024OrbitGrowthSoficPFT}.  The graph-covering context
is represented by \cite{StarkTerras1996FiniteGraphsCoverings}.  The short
inverse-data appendix uses Boyle--Schmieding's conjugacy-class periodic data
and the gain-graph literature
\cite{BoyleSchmieding2017FiniteGroupExtensions,
CavaleriDAngeliDonno2021BalanceGainGraphs,CavaleriDonno2022Cospectrality,
AbiadBelardoKhramova2024Switching,Zaslavsky1989BiasedGraphs,
GrossTucker1987TopologicalGraphTheory}.

\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}

Let \(\Gamma\) be a finite directed multigraph with primitive adjacency
matrix \(A\), Perron root \(\lambda>1\), and one-sided edge shift
\(X_\Gamma^+\).  A map \(\tau:E(\Gamma)\to G\) to a finite group defines a
one-step skew-product cocycle.  If \(\gamma\) is a primitive periodic orbit,
the ordered product of its edge labels is defined up to conjugacy; write
\([g_\gamma]\) for that conjugacy class.  For a conjugacy class \(C\subset G\)
we consider
\begin{equation}\label{eq:intro-class-product}
  P_C(N):=
  \prod_{\substack{\gamma\text{ primitive}\\
                    |\gamma|\leq N,\ [g_\gamma]=C}}
       \left(1-\lambda^{-|\gamma|}\right).
\end{equation}
The dynamical-zeta and periodic-orbit framework is classical
\cite{BowenLanford1970Zeta,Parry1983PrimeOrbit,ParryPollicott1990Zeta}.
For a finite-group extension with the required extension-level aperiodicity,
the exponent in the Mertens asymptotic for \eqref{eq:intro-class-product} is
\(|C|/|G|\).  Mixing of the base alone does not supply that hypothesis.  The
correction below concerns both this missing hypothesis and the constant that
is obtained once it holds.

For a class function \(\phi\) on \(G\), put
\[
  \Pi_\phi(z):=\sum_{\gamma\text{ primitive}}
     \phi(g_\gamma)z^{|\gamma|}.
\]
There are two natural logarithmic transforms of this primitive series:
\begin{align}
  L_\phi(z)
    &:=\sum_{r\geq1}\frac{\Pi_{\psi^r\phi}(z^r)}{r},
    & (\psi^r\phi)(g)&:=\phi(g^r),
    \label{eq:intro-artin}\\
  F_\phi(z)
    &:=\sum_{r\geq1}\frac{\Pi_\phi(z^r)}{r}.
    \label{eq:intro-fixed}
\end{align}
The first is the periodic Artin logarithm: the \(r\)-fold traversal changes
the primitive label from \(g_\gamma\) to \(g_\gamma^r\).  The second is the
fixed-label Euler transform: the scalar factor in
\(\log(1-\lambda^{-|\gamma|})\) is repeated, but membership of the primitive
orbit in \(C\) is not re-tested after powering its label.  Accordingly,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:intro-difference}
  F_\phi(z)-L_\phi(z)
  =\sum_{r\geq2}\frac{
       \Pi_\phi(z^r)-\Pi_{\psi^r\phi}(z^r)}{r}.
\end{equation}
This elementary distinction is the source of the correction theorem.

\subsection{Main result and correction of the 1999 constant}

Let \(A_\rho\) be the finite twisted adjacency matrix in an irreducible
unitary representation \(\rho\) of \(G\), and assume the strict twisted gap
\[
  \max_{\rho\neq\mathbf1}\rad(A_\rho)<\lambda.
\]
The gap is a finite, checkable hypothesis; it is not inferred from mixing of
the base alone.  Define
\[
  C(A):=\lim_{t\uparrow1}(1-t)\det(I-tA/\lambda)^{-1}>0.
\]
The central theorem, proved in \Cref{sec:product-correction}, is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:intro-main-constant}
  P_C(N)=K_C N^{-|C|/|G|}\bigl(1+O(N^{-1})\bigr),
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}\label{eq:intro-kc}
  K_C=
  \exp\!\left\{-\frac{|C|}{|G|}
    \left(\gamma+\log C(A)
      +\sum_{\rho\neq\mathbf1}
        \overline{\chi_\rho(C)}F_{\chi_\rho}(\lambda^{-1})
    \right)\right\}.
\end{equation}
Moreover, each non-trivial fixed-label value in \eqref{eq:intro-kc} is
recovered from the finite determinant family by Adams--M\"obius inversion.
Thus the new content is not the exponent or the existence scale of the
product, but the corrected finite-state coordinate of its constant.

The correction also has a quotient-cover form.  For every normal subgroup
\(H\triangleleft G\), \Cref{thm:quotient-cover-splitting} factors the product
of the constants for classes in \(H\) into the regular quotient-cover
determinant and an absolutely convergent positive product over primitive
orbits whose quotient lifts require more than one traversal to close.  In
particular, the old Artin coordinate strictly underestimates the
identity-class constant in every non-trivial strict-gap extension.  We also
prove an exponentially accurate harmonic renormalization for every union of
classes.

Mohamed and Noorani state the corresponding exponent and constant under
mixing of the base, with the constant expressed through Artin values
\(L(e^{-h},\chi)\), where \(e^h=\lambda\)
\cite[Theorem~1 and its proof on pp.~124--125]{MeslizaNoorani1999FrobeniusMertens}.
Their character-orthogonality step starts from the correct fixed class
condition.  The next identification with \(\log L(e^{-h},\chi)\), however,
changes the \(r\)-th logarithmic summand from
\(\chi(g_\gamma)\lambda^{-r|\gamma|}/r\) to
\(\chi(g_\gamma^r)\lambda^{-r|\gamma|}/r\).  That substitution is generally
false.  Base mixing also fails to imply uniform Frobenius density: the trivial
\(C_2\)-cocycle in \Cref{rem:trivial-cocycle-counterexample} has a mixing base
but no orbit in the non-identity class.  \Cref{thm:formal-correction} therefore
states two independent repairs.  Under the strict twisted gap, the \(S_3\)
construction in \Cref{sec:s3-witness} gives a direct contradiction to the
constant: the sign Artin logarithm is zero, while the sign fixed-label
coordinate is strictly negative.

The corrected constants do not themselves recover the full primitive data.
\Cref{thm:c2-boundary-collision} gives two strict-gap \(C_2\)-labellings of
the same four-vertex graph for which every class constant agrees although the
length-one class counts differ.  The equality follows from the exact
determinant coboundary \(Q(z^2)/Q(z)^2\), with \(Q(1/4)=1\), rather than from
numerical approximation.  The example is minimal in the regular polynomial
certificate class of \Cref{prop:c2-certificate-minimality}.  Within the
same-base strict-gap determinant-ratio class,
\Cref{thm:determinant-boundary-lifting} proves that these are all the isolated
boundary collisions: vanishing of the single boundary functional is equivalent
to a unique normalized rational Mahler certificate.  The proof uses
determinant parity together with Nishioka's published critical special-value
theorem, and does not assert the corresponding converse for arbitrary positive
rational functions.  The resulting rational certificate is fully effective:
\Cref{thm:effective-rational-mahler-coboundary} gives explicit degree and
height bounds and a complete finite Pad\'e decision procedure, while
\Cref{cor:same-base-effective-rational-mahler} specializes the input bounds to
compatible matrices on one base.  That effectiveness has an inverse
consequence: \Cref{thm:finite-radial-sampling} proves that a non-trivial
same-base \(C_2\) pair has at most \(N-1\) algebraic radial collision points,
where \(N\) is the certificate degree bound, and that
\(N_*<16v^3\) prescribed algebraic samples recover the complete profile on a
\(v\)-vertex base.  Finally, \Cref{thm:radial-profile-recovery} proves for
every finite group that equality on any set with an interior accumulation
point recovers all primitive length--class data.  No optimality assertion is
made for that general-group analytic hypothesis.

\subsection{Scope of the background appendix}

Equality of all represented spectra is the finite-group gain-graph notion of
\(G\)-cospectrality, and its translation to conjugacy-class periodic data is
established context
\cite{CavaleriDAngeliDonno2021BalanceGainGraphs,
CavaleriDonno2022Cospectrality,BoyleSchmieding2017FiniteGroupExtensions}.
Switching and spanning-tree normalization are likewise standard
\cite{Zaslavsky1989BiasedGraphs,GrossTucker1987TopologicalGraphTheory};
non-switching-equivalent cospectral constructions form an active gain-graph
literature \cite{AbiadBelardoKhramova2024Switching}.

Appendix~\ref{sec:inverse-background} records only the determinant/periodic-data
dictionary and the standard finite fundamental-cycle switching certificate.
No novelty or dynamical inverse classification is claimed for these facts.

\subsection{Position in the literature}

The determinant formula for shifts of finite type and the general
periodic-orbit/zeta formalism are standard
\cite{BowenLanford1970Zeta,Parry1983PrimeOrbit,ParryPollicott1990Zeta}.
Likewise, M\"obius inversion between primitive Euler coordinates and periodic
ghost coordinates is classical necklace/Witt calculus
\cite{MetropolisRota1983Necklaces}.  Noorani's Artin-function treatment
\cite{Noorani1995ChebotarevFiniteExtensions} precedes the explicit
Frobenius-class product formula of Mohamed and Noorani
\cite{MeslizaNoorani1999FrobeniusMertens}.  The contribution here is the
identification of the fixed primitive-label coordinate selected by that
product, followed by application of the classical transform to the finite
family of twisted determinant blocks.  The distinction is also relevant when
the 1999 theorem is invoked in later orbit-growth work; the precise effect on
\cite{NordinNooraniMohd2024OrbitGrowthSoficPFT} is stated in
\Cref{rem:post-1999-scope}.  The graph-covering Artin-zeta context may be
found in \cite{StarkTerras1996FiniteGraphsCoverings}.
The arithmetic Chebotarev Mertens theorem likewise requires auxiliary local
factors beyond Artin values
\cite{ArangoPinerosKeliherKeyes2022ChebotarevMertens}; it is an analogue, not
the dynamical quotient-cover identity proved here.
The scalar all-order Bernoulli expansion for graph-theoretic Mertens sums is
due to Hasegawa and Saito \cite{HasegawaSaito2016GraphMertens}; our refinement
does not claim those scalar coefficients as new, but supplies the exact
harmonic normalization and exponential remainder for all fixed-label unions
under the twisted gap.

The nearest algorithmic Mahler results compute solutions of linear Mahler
operators \cite{ChyzakDreyfusDumasMezzarobba2018MahlerSolutions} or decide
additive rational summability by Mahler discrete residues
\cite{ArrecheZhang2022MahlerResidues}.  Pegis treats a different nonlinear
functional equation with constant coefficients
\cite{Pegis1996NonlinearMahler}.  None of these results gives the normalized
multiplicative coboundary bounds used here or the resulting finite-sample
dynamical inverse theorem.

\subsection{Organization}

Section~\ref{sec:framework} fixes the determinant, Adams, and boundary
conventions.  Section~\ref{sec:product-correction} proves the corrected
theorem, including its necessary gap hypothesis, and identifies the precise
constant error in the 1999 proof.
Section~\ref{sec:quotient-refinements} proves the quotient-cover obstruction,
and the harmonic refinement.
Section~\ref{sec:s3-witness} gives the exact \(S_3\) contradiction.
Section~\ref{sec:boundary-collisions} proves non-injectivity of the complete
class-constant vector, certifies a finite minimality statement, and gives
effective finite-sample binary recovery together with a general radial-profile
recovery criterion.
Section~\ref{sec:exactness} records the exact algebraic gap certificate and
reproducibility information.  Two short appendices contain the general radial
boundary convention and established periodic-data/gain-graph background.

\section{Finite-state framework and the two logarithms}\label{sec:framework}

\subsection{One-sided edge shifts and twisted determinants}

Throughout, \(\Gamma=(V,E,o,t)\) is a finite directed multigraph.  Parallel
edges are allowed and are regarded as named edges.  Its adjacency matrix is
\[
  A_{ij}=\#\{e\in E:o(e)=i,\ t(e)=j\}.
\]
We assume that \(A\) is primitive and that its Perron root satisfies
\(\lambda>1\).  The phase space in every main statement is the one-sided
edge shift
\[
  X_\Gamma^+=\{(e_0,e_1,\ldots):t(e_j)=o(e_{j+1})\},
  \qquad \sigma(e_0e_1\ldots)=e_1e_2\ldots.
\]
No assertion about arbitrary two-sided cocycles is implicit in this
notation.

Fix a finite group \(G\) and an edge labelling \(\tau:E\to G\).  For a
directed path \(q=e_0\cdots e_{n-1}\), set
\(\tau(q)=\tau(e_0)\cdots\tau(e_{n-1})\).  Changing the initial point on a
closed path conjugates this product.  Hence a periodic orbit \(\gamma\) has a
well-defined Frobenius conjugacy class \([g_\gamma]\), although a representative
\(g_\gamma\) requires a base point.

For a finite-dimensional unitary representation
\(\rho:G\to U(V_\rho)\), define the twisted adjacency matrix by blocks
\begin{equation}\label{eq:twisted-adjacency}
  (A_\rho)_{ij}:=\sum_{e:i\to j}\rho(\tau(e))
  \quad\in\End(V_\rho),
  \qquad
  P_\rho(z):=\det(I-zA_\rho).
\end{equation}
For the trivial representation, \(A_{\mathbf1}=A\).

\begin{lemma}[Two-sided left cocycles and chronological edge products]
\label{lem:convention-bridge}
Let \((\bar X_A,\sigma)\) be a two-sided shift of finite type and let
\[
  \widetilde\sigma(x,g)=(\sigma x,\alpha(x)g)
\]
be a continuous cocycle with values in the finite group \(G\).  After a
higher-block recoding, \(\alpha\) is a one-step function on directed edges.
Let \(X_A^+\) be the corresponding one-sided edge shift and set
\(\tau(e)=\alpha(e)^{-1}\).  The two-sided and one-sided primitive periodic
orbits correspond to the same cyclic edge words, with the same least period.
For a based word \(\gamma=e_0\cdots e_{n-1}\), its left-cocycle holonomy and
chronological edge product satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:convention-holonomy-inverse}
 h_\gamma=\alpha(e_{n-1})\cdots\alpha(e_0),
 \qquad
 g_\gamma=\tau(e_0)\cdots\tau(e_{n-1})=h_\gamma^{-1}.
\end{equation}
Consequently, for every conjugacy class \(C\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:convention-class-counts}
 p^{\mathrm{left}}_{n,C}=p^{\mathrm{edge}}_{n,C^{-1}},
 \qquad
 P^{\mathrm{left}}_C(N)=P^{\mathrm{edge}}_{C^{-1}}(N).
\end{equation}
If \(\bar\rho\) denotes the contragredient of the unitary representation
\(\rho\), then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:convention-character-blocks}
 \chi_\rho(h_\gamma)=\chi_{\bar\rho}(g_\gamma),
 \qquad
 P^{\mathrm{left}}_\rho(z)=P^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\bar\rho}(z).
\end{equation}
Here the left-convention block is defined after recoding by
\(B^{\mathrm{left}}_\rho:=A^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\bar\rho,\tau}\) and
\(P^{\mathrm{left}}_\rho(z):=\det(I-zB^{\mathrm{left}}_\rho)\); its
length-\(n\) trace is the sum of \(\chi_\rho(h_\gamma)\) over based
periodic words.  In particular,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:convention-channel-transport}
 \Pi^{\mathrm{left}}_{\chi_\rho}
   =\Pi^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\chi_{\bar\rho}},\qquad
 L^{\mathrm{left}}_{\chi_\rho}
   =L^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\chi_{\bar\rho}},\qquad
 F^{\mathrm{left}}_{\chi_\rho}
   =F^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\chi_{\bar\rho}}.
\end{equation}
Thus the Fourier formulas are transported by
\(C\mapsto C^{-1}\) and \(\rho\mapsto\bar\rho\).  Conjugate representation
pairs give the same real class-product constant under this transport.
Every logarithm in this correspondence is the germ normalized at \(z=0\),
continued along the radial segment \(0\le z\le\lambda^{-1}\) whenever an
endpoint value is used.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Continuity into the finite discrete group makes \(\alpha\) locally constant;
a higher-block conjugacy therefore makes it depend on one edge and preserves
least periods.  Iterating the left cocycle gives the reverse-ordered product
\(h_\gamma\), and the definition of \(\tau\) gives
\eqref{eq:convention-holonomy-inverse}.  Cyclic changes of base point conjugate
both products, which proves \eqref{eq:convention-class-counts}.  Since
\(\chi_\rho(g^{-1})=\overline{\chi_\rho(g)}=\chi_{\bar\rho}(g)\), the weighted
periodic traces agree after \(\rho\mapsto\bar\rho\).  The trace-determinant
identity then gives \eqref{eq:convention-character-blocks}; its normalized
germ fixes the stated logarithm branch.
\end{proof}

Let \(N_{n,C}\) be the number of period-\(n\) points whose length-\(n\)
holonomy lies in the conjugacy class \(C\).  Let \(p_{n,C}\) be the number of
primitive periodic orbits of least period \(n\) with Frobenius class \(C\).
For a class function \(\phi:G\to\CC\), use the abbreviations
\begin{align}
  T_n(\phi)&:=\sum_C\phi(C)N_{n,C},
  &c_n(\phi)&:=\sum_C\phi(C)p_{n,C},\label{eq:weighted-counts}\\
  \Pi_\phi(z)&:=\sum_{n\geq1}c_n(\phi)z^n
  =\sum_{\gamma\in\cP}\phi(g_\gamma)z^{|\gamma|}.
  \label{eq:primitive-channel}
\end{align}

\begin{lemma}[Perron-weighted domination of twisted blocks]
\label{lem:perron-weighted-domination}
Let \(v>0\) satisfy \(Av=\lambda v\), and put
\(D_\rho=|V|\dim\rho\).  On block vectors \(x=(x_i)_{i\in V}\), define
\[
  \norm{x}_v:=\max_{i\in V}\frac{\norm{x_i}}{v_i}.
\]
Then, for every unitary representation \(\rho\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:perron-weighted-block-bound}
  \norm{A_\rho}_v\le\lambda,
  \qquad \rad(A_\rho)\le\lambda,
  \qquad \abs{\Tr(A_\rho^n)}\le D_\rho\lambda^n
  \quad(n\ge1).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Unitarity gives
\[
 \norm{(A_\rho x)_i}
 \le \sum_j\sum_{e:i\to j}\norm{\rho(\tau(e))x_j}
 \le \sum_j A_{ij}v_j\norm{x}_v
 =\lambda v_i\norm{x}_v.
\]
Hence \(\norm{A_\rho}_v\le\lambda\) and
\(\rad(A_\rho)\le\lambda\).  Every eigenvalue of \(A_\rho\) has modulus at
most \(\lambda\); summing the \(n\)-th powers of the \(D_\rho\) eigenvalues,
with algebraic multiplicity, proves the trace bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Twisted trace and periodic logarithm]\label{lem:trace-log}
For every irreducible representation \(\rho\) and every \(n\geq1\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:twisted-trace}
  \Tr(A_\rho^n)=T_n(\chi_\rho).
\end{equation}
Consequently, on \(|z|<\lambda^{-1}\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:artin-determinant-log}
  L_{\chi_\rho}(z):=-\Log_0P_\rho(z)
  =\sum_{n\geq1}\frac{T_n(\chi_\rho)}{n}z^n
  =\sum_{r\geq1}\frac{\Pi_{\psi^r\chi_\rho}(z^r)}{r}.
\end{equation}
Here \(\Log_0P_\rho\) is the analytic germ at zero normalized by
\(\Log_0P_\rho(0)=0\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Expanding the block product in \(A_\rho^n\) sums \(\rho(\tau(q))\) over
closed length-\(n\) paths, with a trace over \(V_\rho\); this is
  \eqref{eq:twisted-trace}.  The finite-matrix identity
\[
  -\Log_0\det(I-zB)=\sum_{n\geq1}\Tr(B^n)z^n/n
\]
gives the second expression in \eqref{eq:artin-determinant-log}.  A primitive
orbit \(\gamma\) of length \(d\) contributes its \(d\) cyclic base points to
period \(n=rd\), and its repeated holonomy is \(g_\gamma^r\).  Division by
  \(n=rd\) leaves the factor \(1/r\), proving the last expression.  Absolute
  convergence follows from \Cref{lem:perron-weighted-domination} on every
  smaller disc.
\end{proof}

For a general class function, write
\[
  \phi=\sum_{\rho\in\Irr(G)}
     \widehat\phi(\rho)\chi_\rho,
  \qquad
  \widehat\phi(\rho)
    =\frac1{|G|}\sum_{g\in G}\phi(g)\overline{\chi_\rho(g)},
\]
and define \(L_\phi=\sum_\rho\widehat\phi(\rho)L_{\chi_\rho}\).  This agrees
with the orbit expansion in \eqref{eq:artin-determinant-log}.  The Adams
operation \(\psi^m\) acts on class functions by
\((\psi^m\phi)(g)=\phi(g^m)\).  It need not preserve irreducibility or
positivity, but it has a finite character expansion
\begin{equation}\label{eq:adams-coefficients}
  \psi^m\chi_\rho=\sum_{\pi\in\Irr(G)}
      a_{\rho,\pi}^{(m)}\chi_\pi,
  \qquad
  a_{\rho,\pi}^{(m)}
     =\langle\psi^m\chi_\rho,\chi_\pi\rangle_G.
\end{equation}
The coefficients are periodic in \(m\) modulo the exponent of \(G\), and
therefore uniformly bounded.

\subsection{Adams--M\"obius primitive inversion}

Primitive Euler coordinates and periodic ghost coordinates are related by the
classical necklace/Witt M\"obius transform
\cite{MetropolisRota1983Necklaces}.  The point specific to the present problem
is that a Frobenius-class product retains the fixed primitive label before the
Adams power operation is applied.  Applying the classical transform to this
fixed-label coordinate and then to the finite twisted-determinant family gives
the formulas below.

\begin{theorem}[Primitive and fixed-label determinant coordinates]
\label{thm:adams-mobius}
For every class function \(\phi\) and \(|z|<\lambda^{-1}\),
\begin{align}
  \Pi_\phi(z)
    &=\sum_{m\geq1}\frac{\mu(m)}{m}
       L_{\psi^m\phi}(z^m),
       \label{eq:primitive-adams-mobius}\\
  F_\phi(z):=\sum_{r\geq1}\frac{\Pi_\phi(z^r)}{r}
    &=\sum_{r,m\geq1}\frac{\mu(m)}{rm}
       L_{\psi^m\phi}(z^{rm}).
       \label{eq:fixed-adams-mobius}
\end{align}
For \(\phi=\chi_\rho\), insertion of \eqref{eq:adams-coefficients} gives a
formula involving only the finite determinant family:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fixed-determinant-open-disc}
  F_{\chi_\rho}(z)=
  \sum_{r,m\geq1}\frac{\mu(m)}{rm}
    \sum_{\pi\in\Irr(G)}a_{\rho,\pi}^{(m)}
      \bigl(-\Log_0P_\pi(z^{rm})\bigr).
\end{equation}
All three series are absolutely convergent on compact subsets of the Perron
disc after expansion into primitive orbits.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{lem:trace-log},
\[
  L_{\psi^m\phi}(z^m)
  =\sum_{\gamma\in\cP}\sum_{r\geq1}
     \frac{\phi(g_\gamma^{mr})}{r}z^{mr|\gamma|}.
\]
Multiplying by \(\mu(m)/m\), summing, and putting \(s=mr\), the coefficient
of \(\phi(g_\gamma^s)z^{s|\gamma|}\) becomes
\[
  \sum_{m\mid s}\frac{\mu(m)}{m(s/m)}
  =\frac1s\sum_{m\mid s}\mu(m).
\]
It is one for \(s=1\) and zero otherwise.  This proves
\eqref{eq:primitive-adams-mobius}.  Summing that identity over \(z^r/r\)
proves \eqref{eq:fixed-adams-mobius}.  Finally use the character expansion
\eqref{eq:adams-coefficients} and \eqref{eq:artin-determinant-log}.

For convergence, the total number \(p_n\) of primitive length-\(n\) orbits
satisfies \(p_n\leq\Tr(A^n)/n\ll\lambda^n/n\).  Thus the orbit expansions
are dominated on \(|z|\leq R<\lambda^{-1}\) by convergent logarithmic series.
This also justifies every rearrangement above.
\end{proof}

The difference between the periodic and fixed-label transforms is now
formal rather than terminological.

\begin{proposition}[Exact Artin-substitution obstruction]
\label{prop:artin-substitution-obstruction}
On \(|z|<\lambda^{-1}\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:exact-f-minus-l}
  F_\phi(z)-L_\phi(z)
  =\sum_{r\geq2}\frac{
       \Pi_\phi(z^r)-\Pi_{\psi^r\phi}(z^r)}{r}.
\end{equation}
Writing \(\Pi_\phi(z)=\sum_{n\geq1}c_n(\phi)z^n\), the two germs agree if
and only if
\begin{equation}\label{eq:coefficient-obstruction}
  \sum_{r\mid n}\frac1r
    c_{n/r}(\phi-\psi^r\phi)=0
  \qquad(n\geq1).
\end{equation}
There is no general character-theoretic reason for these identities to hold.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Subtract the two orbit expansions in
\eqref{eq:intro-artin}--\eqref{eq:intro-fixed}; the \(r=1\) terms cancel.
Extracting the coefficient of \(z^n\) gives
\eqref{eq:coefficient-obstruction}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The Perron boundary is derived from the determinant}

Put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:strict-gap}
  \eta:=\max_{\rho\in\Irr(G),\ \rho\neq\mathbf1}\rad(A_\rho),
  \qquad \max\varnothing:=0.
\end{equation}
The main product theorem assumes \(\eta<\lambda\).  Under this hypothesis,
the \(rm=1\) terms in \eqref{eq:fixed-determinant-open-disc} are regular for
non-trivial \(\pi\), and every term with \(rm\geq2\) is an interior value
because
\begin{equation}\label{eq:interior-radius}
  \rad(\lambda^{-rm}A_\pi)\leq\lambda^{1-rm}<1.
\end{equation}

Only the zero-free radial endpoint is used below.  The general peripheral
factorization and renormalized boundary construction are recorded separately
in Appendix~\ref{app:radial-boundary}.

\begin{lemma}[Perron evaluation of a non-trivial fixed-label coordinate]
\label{lem:perron-fixed-coordinate}
Assume \(\eta<\lambda\).  For every non-trivial irreducible \(\rho\), the
series
\begin{equation}\label{eq:perron-fixed-determinant}
  \mathfrak F_\rho:=
  \sum_{r,m\geq1}\frac{\mu(m)}{rm}
    \sum_{\substack{\pi\in\Irr(G)\\(r,m,\pi)\neq(1,1,\mathbf1)}}
       a_{\rho,\pi}^{(m)}
       \bigl(-\Log_{[0,1]}P_\pi(\lambda^{-rm})\bigr)
\end{equation}
is well defined and equals \(F_{\chi_\rho}(\lambda^{-1})\).  In
\eqref{eq:perron-fixed-determinant}, the notation means radial continuation
for the finitely many terms with \(rm=1\) and the normalized interior germ
for \(rm\geq2\).  The omitted scalar Perron triple has coefficient
\(a_{\rho,\mathbf1}^{(1)}=0\) and is deleted before endpoint evaluation.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Since \(\rho\neq\mathbf1\), character orthogonality gives
\(a_{\rho,\mathbf1}^{(1)}=0\).  If \(rm=1\), all remaining \(\pi\) are
non-trivial and \(P_\pi(t/\lambda)\neq0\) for \(0\leq t\leq1\) by the strict
gap.  For \(rm\geq2\), the trace expansion and
\(\abs{\Tr(A_\pi^n)}\leq(\dim A_\pi)\lambda^n\) give, uniformly for
\(0\leq t\leq1\),
\[
 \abs{-\Log_0P_\pi(t^{rm}\lambda^{-rm})}
 \leq C\sum_{n\geq1}\frac{\lambda^{(1-rm)n}}n
 \leq C'\lambda^{1-rm}.
\]
The Adams coefficients are uniformly bounded, and
\[
  \sum_{rm\geq2}\frac{\lambda^{1-rm}}{rm}
  =\sum_{k\geq2}\frac{d(k)}k\lambda^{1-k}<\infty.
\]
Dominated convergence in \eqref{eq:fixed-determinant-open-disc}, along
\(z=t/\lambda\), proves the assertion.
\end{proof}


[32m succeeded in 151ms:[0m
\section{The corrected Frobenius-class product constant}
\label{sec:product-correction}

We first isolate the scalar normalization.  Let
\(p_n=\sum_Cp_{n,C}\) and
\(\Pi_{\mathbf1}(z)=\sum_{n\geq1}p_nz^n\).  Define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scalar-finite-part}
  \mathcal F(A):=
  \sum_{n\geq1}\left(p_n\lambda^{-n}-\frac1n\right),
  \qquad
  \mathcal M(A):=\gamma+\mathcal F(A).
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}[Scalar and class Mertens sums]\label{lem:class-mertens}
The series in \eqref{eq:scalar-finite-part} converges absolutely, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scalar-mertens}
  \sum_{n\leq N}p_n\lambda^{-n}
  =\log N+\mathcal M(A)+O(N^{-1}).
\end{equation}
Moreover,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scalar-c-relation}
  \mathcal F(A)+
  \sum_{r\geq2}\frac{\Pi_{\mathbf1}(\lambda^{-r})}{r}
  =\log C(A),
  \qquad
  C(A)=\lim_{t\uparrow1}(1-t)\det(I-tA/\lambda)^{-1}.
\end{equation}

If the strict gap \(\eta<\lambda\) holds, then for every conjugacy class
\(C\subset G\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:class-mertens}
  \sum_{n\leq N}p_{n,C}\lambda^{-n}
  =\frac{|C|}{|G|}\log N+\mathcal M_C+O(N^{-1}),
\end{equation}
uniformly over the finite set of conjugacy classes, where
\begin{equation}\label{eq:class-mertens-constant}
  \mathcal M_C=
  \frac{|C|}{|G|}\left(
    \mathcal M(A)+
    \sum_{\rho\neq\mathbf1}
       \overline{\chi_\rho(C)}\Pi_{\chi_\rho}(\lambda^{-1})
  \right).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
M\"obius inversion of
\(\Tr(A^n)=\sum_{d\mid n}d p_d\) gives
\[
  p_n=\frac1n\sum_{d\mid n}\mu(d)\Tr(A^{n/d}).
\]
If \(\theta<\lambda\) bounds the non-Perron spectrum of \(A\), then
\(\Tr(A^n)=\lambda^n+O(\theta^n)\).  The term \(d=1\) therefore gives
\(p_n\lambda^{-n}=n^{-1}+O((\theta/\lambda)^n/n)\), while the divisors
\(d\geq2\) contribute \(O(d(n)\lambda^{-n/2}/n)\).  This proves absolute
convergence in \eqref{eq:scalar-finite-part}; comparison with
\(H_N=\log N+\gamma+O(N^{-1})\) proves \eqref{eq:scalar-mertens}.

The scalar Euler identity is
\[
  -\Log_0\det(I-zA)
  =\sum_{r\geq1}\frac{\Pi_{\mathbf1}(z^r)}r.
\]
Put \(z=t/\lambda\), add \(\log(1-t)\), and let \(t\uparrow1\).  The
\(r=1\) term tends to \(\mathcal F(A)\), because
\(\sum_{n\geq1}t^n/n=-\log(1-t)\); the \(r\geq2\) terms pass to the limit by
absolute convergence.  This proves \eqref{eq:scalar-c-relation}.

For the class statement, character orthogonality gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:class-indicator}
  \mathbf1_C(g)=\frac{|C|}{|G|}
     \sum_{\rho\in\Irr(G)}
        \overline{\chi_\rho(C)}\chi_\rho(g).
\end{equation}
Hence
\[
  p_{n,C}=\frac{|C|}{|G|}
   \left(p_n+\sum_{\rho\neq\mathbf1}
     \overline{\chi_\rho(C)}c_n(\chi_\rho)\right).
\]
Coefficient extraction in \eqref{eq:primitive-adams-mobius} gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:primitive-coefficient-bound-start}
  c_n(\chi_\rho)=\frac1n\sum_{m\mid n}\mu(m)
       T_{n/m}(\psi^m\chi_\rho).
\end{equation}
Let
\[
 a_*:=\max_{\rho,\pi,m}\abs{a_{\rho,\pi}^{(m)}},
 \qquad D_*:=\max_{\pi\in\Irr(G)}|V|\dim\pi.
\]
The maximum defining \(a_*\) is finite because the Adams coefficients are
periodic in \(m\).  The trace bound in
\Cref{lem:perron-weighted-domination} and the definition of \(\eta\) give the
explicit estimate
\begin{align}
 \abs{c_n(\chi_\rho)}
 &\le \frac{D_\rho\eta^n}{n}
   +\frac{a_*|\Irr(G)|D_*}{n}
       \sum_{\substack{m\mid n\\m\ge2}}\lambda^{n/m}
       \label{eq:explicit-primitive-trace-bound}\\
 &\le \frac{D_\rho\eta^n+
       a_*|\Irr(G)|D_*d(n)\lambda^{n/2}}{n}.
\end{align}
Thus \(\sum_n\abs{c_n(\chi_\rho)}\lambda^{-n}\) converges with an
exponentially decreasing tail.  Summing the class-indicator identity and
using \eqref{eq:scalar-mertens} proves
\eqref{eq:class-mertens}--\eqref{eq:class-mertens-constant}.
\end{proof}

We can now state the product theorem in its focused form.

\begin{theorem}[Adams-corrected Frobenius-class product constant]
\label{thm:correct-product}
Let \(A\) be primitive with Perron root \(\lambda>1\), let
\(\tau:E(\Gamma)\to G\), and assume the certified strict twisted gap
\(\eta<\lambda\).  For every conjugacy class \(C\subset G\), the product
\eqref{eq:intro-class-product} satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:correct-product-asymptotic}
  P_C(N)=K_C N^{-|C|/|G|}\bigl(1+O(N^{-1})\bigr),
\end{equation}
with an error uniform in \(C\).  The positive constant is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:correct-product-orbit}
  K_C=\exp\!\left(
    -\mathcal M_C-
    \sum_{r\geq2}\frac{\Pi_{\mathbf1_C}(\lambda^{-r})}{r}
  \right),
\end{equation}
and equivalently
\begin{equation}\label{eq:correct-product-fourier}
  K_C=\exp\!\left\{-\frac{|C|}{|G|}
    \left(\gamma+\log C(A)+
      \sum_{\rho\neq\mathbf1}
       \overline{\chi_\rho(C)}F_{\chi_\rho}(\lambda^{-1})
    \right)\right\}.
\end{equation}
Finally, the fixed-label values in \eqref{eq:correct-product-fourier} are
the Adams-corrected finite-determinant coordinates
\begin{equation}\label{eq:correct-product-determinant-coordinate}
 F_{\chi_\rho}(\lambda^{-1})=\mathfrak F_\rho,
\end{equation}
where \(\mathfrak F_\rho\) is the derived radial expression
\eqref{eq:perron-fixed-determinant}.  Thus neither a branch family nor a
peripheral factorization is an additional input beyond the polynomials,
\(\lambda\), and the fixed radial convention.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Taking real logarithms in \eqref{eq:intro-class-product} gives
\begin{align}
  \log P_C(N)
  &=-\sum_{n\leq N}p_{n,C}\lambda^{-n}
    -\sum_{r\geq2}\frac1r
       \sum_{n\leq N}p_{n,C}\lambda^{-rn}.
       \label{eq:product-log-split}
\end{align}
The first term is handled by \eqref{eq:class-mertens}.  Since
\(0\leq p_{n,C}\leq p_n\ll\lambda^n/n\), the second double series converges
absolutely and its omitted tail is \(O(\lambda^{-N}/N)\), uniformly in
\(C\).  Therefore
\[
  \log P_C(N)=-\frac{|C|}{|G|}\log N+\log K_C+O(N^{-1}),
\]
with \(K_C\) as in \eqref{eq:correct-product-orbit}.  Exponentiation proves
\eqref{eq:correct-product-asymptotic}.

Apply the class-indicator expansion \eqref{eq:class-indicator} first to
\(\mathcal M_C\) and then to the absolutely convergent \(r\geq2\) tail.
The trivial coordinate becomes
\[
 \mathcal M(A)+\sum_{r\geq2}\Pi_{\mathbf1}(\lambda^{-r})/r
 =\gamma+\log C(A)
\]
by \eqref{eq:scalar-c-relation}.  For \(\rho\neq\mathbf1\), the corresponding
coordinate is
\[
 \Pi_{\chi_\rho}(\lambda^{-1})+
 \sum_{r\geq2}\Pi_{\chi_\rho}(\lambda^{-r})/r
 =F_{\chi_\rho}(\lambda^{-1}).
\]
This proves \eqref{eq:correct-product-fourier}.  Complex conjugate
representations pair to make the bracket real; positivity is also immediate
from \eqref{eq:correct-product-orbit}.  The determinant identity
\eqref{eq:correct-product-determinant-coordinate} is
\Cref{lem:perron-fixed-coordinate}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Homogeneous union of Frobenius classes]
\label{cor:homogeneous-union-constant}
Let \(\mathcal C_\ell\) be any collection of conjugacy classes; in the
homogeneous-extension application, these are the classes for which the cyclic
subgroup action has partition type \(\ell\).  Put
\[
 P_\ell(N):=\prod_{C\in\mathcal C_\ell}P_C(N),
 \qquad
 \delta_\ell:=\frac1{|G|}\sum_{C\in\mathcal C_\ell}|C|.
\]
Under the hypotheses of \Cref{thm:correct-product},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:homogeneous-union-product}
 P_\ell(N)=K_\ell N^{-\delta_\ell}\bigl(1+O(N^{-1})\bigr),
\end{equation}
where \(K_\ell=\prod_{C\in\mathcal C_\ell}K_C\) and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:homogeneous-union-constant}
 \begin{split}
 \log K_\ell={}&-\delta_\ell\bigl(\gamma+\log C(A)\bigr)\\
 &-\frac1{|G|}\sum_{\rho\ne\mathbf1}
   \left(\sum_{C\in\mathcal C_\ell}
       |C|\,\overline{\chi_\rho(C)}\right)
       F_{\chi_\rho}(\lambda^{-1}).
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
This is the fixed-label replacement for an Artin-coordinate constant in the
homogeneous-extension theorem.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Multiply the finitely many asymptotics
\eqref{eq:correct-product-asymptotic}.  Summing the logarithms in
\eqref{eq:correct-product-fourier} gives
\eqref{eq:homogeneous-union-constant}.
\end{proof}

The theorem permits a direct audit of the older formula, before any limit is
taken.  For
\[
  \Pi_{\chi,N}(z):=
  \sum_{\substack{\gamma\in\cP\\|\gamma|\leq N}}
     \chi(g_\gamma)z^{|\gamma|},
\]
character orthogonality gives the exact identity
\begin{equation}\label{eq:exact-truncated-product-identity}
  -\log P_C(N)=\frac{|C|}{|G|}
  \sum_{\rho\in\Irr(G)}\overline{\chi_\rho(C)}
    \sum_{r\geq1}\frac{\Pi_{\chi_\rho,N}(\lambda^{-r})}{r}.
\end{equation}
The \(r\)-th term on the right contains \(\chi_\rho(g_\gamma)\), not
\(\chi_\rho(g_\gamma^r)\).

\begin{theorem}[Formal correction of the Mohamed--Noorani constant]
\label{thm:formal-correction}
Let the base matrix be primitive, with Perron root \(\lambda>1\), and assume
the extension-level aperiodicity condition
\begin{equation}\label{eq:historical-correction-gap}
 \max_{\rho\ne\mathbf1}\rad(A_\rho)<\lambda.
\end{equation}
Then the Frobenius-class product has exponent \(|C|/|G|\) and constant
\eqref{eq:correct-product-fourier}; its non-trivial character coordinate is
\(F_{\chi_\rho}(\lambda^{-1})\), not the periodic Artin logarithm
\(L_{\chi_\rho}(\lambda^{-1})\) in general.  Mixing of the base alone implies
neither the exponent \(|C|/|G|\) nor the asserted Artin-coordinate formula.

More precisely, the proof of
\cite[Theorem~1, pp.~124--125]{MeslizaNoorani1999FrobeniusMertens}
correctly expands the indicator of \(C\) by irreducible characters, but then
identifies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mn-fixed-sum}
  \sum_{\gamma\in\cP}\sum_{r\geq1}
     \frac{\chi_\rho(g_\gamma)}{r}
       \lambda^{-r|\gamma|}
\end{equation}
with the logarithm of its Artin \(L\)-function.  Under the definition of that
\(L\)-function in
\cite[p.~121]{MeslizaNoorani1999FrobeniusMertens}, its logarithm is instead
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mn-artin-sum}
  \sum_{\gamma\in\cP}\sum_{r\geq1}
     \frac{\chi_\rho(g_\gamma^r)}{r}
       \lambda^{-r|\gamma|}.
\end{equation}
The substitution \eqref{eq:mn-fixed-sum}\(={}\)
\eqref{eq:mn-artin-sum} is the erroneous step.  Their difference is exactly
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mn-correction-difference}
  F_{\chi_\rho}(\lambda^{-1})-L_{\chi_\rho}(\lambda^{-1})
  =\sum_{r\geq2}\frac{
    \Pi_{\chi_\rho}(\lambda^{-r})-
    \Pi_{\psi^r\chi_\rho}(\lambda^{-r})}{r}.
\end{equation}
Here, and in transporting the formula from the two-sided convention of the
cited article, the correspondence is the one in
\Cref{lem:convention-bridge}: \(C\mapsto C^{-1}\) and
\(\rho\mapsto\bar\rho\).  Every Artin logarithm is the branch normalized at
zero and continued along \(0\le z\le\lambda^{-1}\).  Replacing every
non-principal Artin logarithm in the asserted constant by
the corresponding fixed-label value \(F_{\chi_\rho}(\lambda^{-1})\) yields
\eqref{eq:correct-product-fourier}.  Under
\eqref{eq:historical-correction-gap}, the Euler constant, scalar
reduced-determinant factor, and exponent require no further change.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Equation \eqref{eq:exact-truncated-product-identity} proves that the product
selects the fixed-label sum \eqref{eq:mn-fixed-sum}.  The periodic orbit
expansion in \Cref{lem:trace-log} proves
\eqref{eq:mn-artin-sum}.  Their exact difference is
\Cref{prop:artin-substitution-obstruction}.  The limiting constant and its
scalar normalization are supplied by \Cref{thm:correct-product}.  The
strict-gap \(S_3\) system in \Cref{thm:s3-contradiction} has
\(L_\varepsilon(1/2)=0\) but \(F_\varepsilon(1/2)<0\), so the invalid
  substitution is not a harmless identity at the evaluation point.  This also
  excludes the possibility that the two displayed constants merely use
  different notations for the same number.  The failure of the exponent under
  mixing of the base alone is established by
  \Cref{rem:trivial-cocycle-counterexample}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Mixing of the base does not suffice]
\label{rem:trivial-cocycle-counterexample}
Let the base be the full two-shift, represented by the one-vertex matrix
\(A=[2]\), let \(G=C_2=\{e,s\}\), and label both loops by \(e\).  The base is
mixing and \(\lambda=2\), but no primitive orbit has Frobenius class \(\{s\}\).
Consequently
\[
 p_{n,\{s\}}=0\quad(n\ge1),
 \qquad P_{\{s\}}(N)=1\quad(N\ge1),
\]
whereas the exponent \(|\{s\}|/|C_2|=1/2\) would force decay proportional to
\(N^{-1/2}\).  For the sign representation \(\varepsilon\),
\[
 A_\varepsilon=A,
 \qquad \rad(A_\varepsilon)=2=\lambda.
\]
Thus the regular extension is not transitive and aperiodic, the strict
twisted gap fails, and the printed base-mixing hypothesis cannot imply the
claimed Frobenius density.  This defect is independent of the replacement
of \(L_\rho\) by \(F_\rho\).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Effect on subsequent class-Mertens statements]
\label{rem:post-1999-scope}
The finite homogeneous extension must satisfy an extension-level
transitivity/aperiodicity hypothesis, and its union-of-classes constant is
\eqref{eq:homogeneous-union-constant}.  The sofic-shift discussion in
\cite[Sec.~4.3]{NordinNooraniMohd2024OrbitGrowthSoficPFT} assumes that the
finite-group extension itself is topologically mixing and denotes its
positive constants abstractly; it does not reproduce the non-principal
Artin-coordinate evaluation.  Its displayed exponent and order statements
are therefore not changed by the coordinate correction.  Any evaluation of
those abstract constants through the explicit 1999 Artin formula must,
however, use \eqref{eq:correct-product-fourier} and
\eqref{eq:homogeneous-union-constant} instead.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Extent of the correction]
Under an extension-level strict gap, the correction concerns the
character-dependent constant and not the classical scalar Mertens
normalization.  Under mixing of the base alone, the leading Frobenius density
may also fail, as \Cref{rem:trivial-cocycle-counterexample} shows.  No claim
of priority is made for Frobenius-class Mertens asymptotics or for the
classical necklace/Witt inversion used in their analysis.
\end{remark}

\section{Quotient-cover obstruction and refined constants}
\label{sec:quotient-refinements}

The fixed-label correction has consequences which are not visible from a
single character channel.  We first identify the exact geometric factor
missed by the Artin substitution and then refine the product asymptotic.

\subsection{Normal quotients and the missing split-orbit factor}

Let \(H\triangleleft G\), put \(Q=G/H\) and \(q=|Q|\), and write
\(\tau_Q:E\to Q\) for the quotient cocycle.  The adjacency matrix of the
regular \(Q\)-skew product is denoted by \(\widetilde A_Q\); explicitly, its
vertices are \(V\times Q\), and an edge \(e:i\to j\) gives an edge
\((i,a)\to(j,a\tau_Q(e))\) for each \(a\in Q\).  Define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:quotient-cover-reduced-constant}
 C(\widetilde A_Q)
 :=\lim_{t\uparrow1}(1-t)
       \det(I-t\widetilde A_Q/\lambda)^{-1}.
\end{equation}
Under the strict twisted gap this limit is positive: the regular
representation decomposition shows that the only peripheral block is the
simple Perron block of \(A\).

For a primitive orbit \(\omega\), let
\begin{equation}\label{eq:quotient-holonomy-order}
 q_H(\omega):=\operatorname{ord}_Q(g_\omega H).
\end{equation}
This is independent of the based representative.  Finally, put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:normal-subgroup-product-constant}
 K_H:=\prod_{\substack{C\in\operatorname{Conj}(G)\\C\subset H}}K_C.
\end{equation}

\begin{theorem}[Exact quotient-cover splitting factor]
\label{thm:quotient-cover-splitting}
Assume the hypotheses of \Cref{thm:correct-product}.  For every normal
subgroup \(H\triangleleft G\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:quotient-cover-splitting}
 K_H=
 \left(\frac{e^{-\gamma}}{C(\widetilde A_Q)}\right)^{1/q}
 \prod_{\substack{\omega\in\cP\\q_H(\omega)>1}}
 \left(1-\lambda^{-q_H(\omega)|\omega|}\right)^{-1/q_H(\omega)}.
\end{equation}
The product in \eqref{eq:quotient-cover-splitting} converges absolutely.
If \(K_H^{\mathrm{Art}}\) denotes the candidate obtained by replacing every
fixed-label coordinate by its periodic Artin coordinate, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:quotient-artin-candidate}
 K_H^{\mathrm{Art}}
 =\left(\frac{e^{-\gamma}}{C(\widetilde A_Q)}\right)^{1/q}
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:quotient-artin-positive-error}
 \log\frac{K_H}{K_H^{\mathrm{Art}}}
 =-\sum_{\substack{\omega\in\cP\\q_H(\omega)>1}}
    \frac{1}{q_H(\omega)}
    \log\left(1-\lambda^{-q_H(\omega)|\omega|}\right).
\end{equation}
Consequently, the Artin candidate is strictly smaller than \(K_H\) if and
only if some primitive orbit has \(g_\omega\notin H\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Inflate each irreducible representation of \(Q\) to \(G\).  The regular
character identity gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:normal-subgroup-indicator}
 \mathbf1_H(g)=\frac{1}{q}\sum_{\sigma\in\Irr(Q)}
       (\dim\sigma)\chi_\sigma(gH).
\end{equation}
Summing \eqref{eq:correct-product-fourier} over the conjugacy classes
contained in \(H\), or equivalently applying
\eqref{eq:normal-subgroup-indicator}, yields
\begin{equation}\label{eq:normal-subgroup-fixed-coordinate}
 \log K_H=-\frac{1}{q}\left(
   \gamma+\log C(A)+
   \sum_{\substack{\sigma\in\Irr(Q)\\\sigma\ne\mathbf1}}
      (\dim\sigma)F_{\chi_\sigma}(\lambda^{-1})\right).
\end{equation}

The regular representation decomposition also gives the polynomial identity
\begin{equation}\label{eq:quotient-regular-determinant-factorization}
 \det(I-z\widetilde A_Q)
 =\prod_{\sigma\in\Irr(Q)}P_\sigma(z)^{\dim\sigma}.
\end{equation}
Removing the simple scalar Perron factor and taking the normalized radial
logarithm at \(z=\lambda^{-1}\) gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:quotient-cover-c-logarithm}
 \log C(\widetilde A_Q)=\log C(A)+
 \sum_{\substack{\sigma\in\Irr(Q)\\\sigma\ne\mathbf1}}
   (\dim\sigma)L_{\chi_\sigma}(\lambda^{-1}).
\end{equation}
Replacing \(F\) by \(L\) in
\eqref{eq:normal-subgroup-fixed-coordinate} and using
\eqref{eq:quotient-cover-c-logarithm} proves
\eqref{eq:quotient-artin-candidate}.

Although \(L_{\mathbf1_H}(\lambda^{-1})\) and
\(F_{\mathbf1_H}(\lambda^{-1})\) diverge separately, their radial difference
does not.  For \(0\le z<\lambda^{-1}\), the primitive-orbit expansions give
\begin{align}
 L_{\mathbf1_H}(z)-F_{\mathbf1_H}(z)
 &=\sum_{\omega\in\cP}\sum_{r\ge1}
   \frac{\mathbf1_H(g_\omega^r)-\mathbf1_H(g_\omega)}{r}
   z^{r|\omega|} \notag\\
 &=\sum_{\substack{\omega\in\cP\\q_H(\omega)>1}}
   \sum_{k\ge1}\frac{z^{kq_H(\omega)|\omega|}}
                       {kq_H(\omega)}.
 \label{eq:quotient-radial-difference}
\end{align}
Indeed, an orbit in \(H\) contributes zero, while for an orbit outside
\(H\), \(g_\omega^r\in H\) holds precisely when \(q_H(\omega)\mid r\).
Since \(q_H(\omega)\ge2\) in the last sum and the number of primitive
length-\(n\) orbits is \(O(\lambda^n/n)\), the endpoint series is absolutely
convergent.  Equations
\eqref{eq:normal-subgroup-fixed-coordinate}--
\eqref{eq:quotient-cover-c-logarithm} identify its endpoint with
\(\log(K_H/K_H^{\mathrm{Art}})\).  Summing the inner geometric logarithm
proves \eqref{eq:quotient-artin-positive-error}, hence
\eqref{eq:quotient-cover-splitting}.  Every summand in
\eqref{eq:quotient-artin-positive-error} is non-negative and is positive
exactly for an orbit outside \(H\), proving the last assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Universal failure for the identity class]
\label{cor:universal-identity-artin-failure}
If \(G\ne\{e\}\) and the strict twisted gap holds, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:identity-class-universal-error}
 \frac{K_{\{e\}}}{K_{\{e\}}^{\mathrm{Art}}}
 =\prod_{\substack{\omega\in\cP\\g_\omega\ne e}}
   \left(1-\lambda^{-\operatorname{ord}(g_\omega)|\omega|}\right)^{
      -1/\operatorname{ord}(g_\omega)}>1.
\end{equation}
Thus the Artin-coordinate formula strictly underestimates the identity-class
constant in every non-trivial strict-gap extension.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Take \(H=\{e\}\) in \Cref{thm:quotient-cover-splitting}.  It remains only to
exclude the possibility that every primitive orbit has identity holonomy.
If that occurred, then every period-\(n\) holonomy would be the identity, so
\(\Tr(A_\rho^n)=(\dim\rho)\Tr(A^n)\) for every irreducible \(\rho\) and every
\(n\ge1\).  Equality of all power sums would make the spectrum of \(A_\rho\)
equal to \(\dim\rho\) copies of the spectrum of \(A\).  A non-trivial finite
group has a non-trivial irreducible representation, for which this would give
\(\rad(A_\rho)=\lambda\), contrary to the strict gap.
\end{proof}

The determinant factorization
\eqref{eq:quotient-regular-determinant-factorization} is standard in
graph-covering Artin theory \cite{StarkTerras1996FiniteGraphsCoverings}.  The
need for auxiliary local correction factors has an arithmetic analogue in
Mertens theorems for Chebotarev prime sets
\cite{ArangoPinerosKeliherKeyes2022ChebotarevMertens}.  The assertion here is
the dynamical critical fixed-label factor
\eqref{eq:quotient-cover-splitting}, expressed geometrically through primitive
base orbits whose quotient lifts close only after more than one traversal.

\subsection{Harmonic renormalization and the complete algebraic jet}

Let \(\mathscr U\subseteq G\) be conjugacy invariant, and define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:union-class-product}
 \alpha_{\mathscr U}:=\frac{|\mathscr U|}{|G|},\qquad
 P_{\mathscr U}(N):=
 \prod_{\substack{\omega\in\cP\\|\omega|\le N,\ g_\omega\in\mathscr U}}
   (1-\lambda^{-|\omega|}),\qquad
 K_{\mathscr U}:=\prod_{C\subset\mathscr U}K_C.
\end{equation}
Here and below \(C\subset\mathscr U\) ranges over conjugacy classes.  Put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:refined-exponential-rate}
 \theta:=\max_{\substack{\nu\in\spec(A)\\\nu\ne\lambda}}|\nu|,
 \qquad
 q_0:=\max\left\{\frac{\theta}{\lambda},
                   \frac{\eta}{\lambda},\lambda^{-1/2}\right\}<1,
\end{equation}
with the maximum over an empty spectrum equal to zero.

\begin{theorem}[Exponentially accurate harmonic renormalization]
\label{thm:harmonic-renormalization}
Under the hypotheses of \Cref{thm:correct-product}, for every conjugacy
invariant \(\mathscr U\subseteq G\) and every \(q_0<\xi<1\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:harmonic-renormalized-product}
 P_{\mathscr U}(N)=K_{\mathscr U}N^{-\alpha_{\mathscr U}}
 \exp\left\{-\alpha_{\mathscr U}
   (H_N-\log N-\gamma)\right\}
 \left(1+O_\xi(\xi^N)\right).
\end{equation}
Consequently,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:harmonic-logarithmic-jet}
 \log\frac{P_{\mathscr U}(N)N^{\alpha_{\mathscr U}}}{K_{\mathscr U}}
 \sim-\frac{\alpha_{\mathscr U}}{2N}
 +\alpha_{\mathscr U}\sum_{j\ge1}
    \frac{B_{2j}}{2jN^{2j}},
\end{equation}
and, in particular,
\begin{align}
 P_{\mathscr U}(N)
 =K_{\mathscr U}N^{-\alpha_{\mathscr U}}
 \bigg(&1-\frac{\alpha_{\mathscr U}}{2N}
 +\frac{\alpha_{\mathscr U}(3\alpha_{\mathscr U}+2)}{24N^2}
 \notag\\
 &-\frac{\alpha_{\mathscr U}^2(\alpha_{\mathscr U}+2)}{48N^3}
 +O(N^{-4})\bigg).
 \label{eq:harmonic-product-jet-three}
\end{align}
Thus all algebraic-order coefficients depend on the system only through
\(\alpha_{\mathscr U}\); the remaining system dependence is in
\(K_{\mathscr U}\) and an exponentially small error.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let \(p_{n,\mathscr U}\) count the primitive length-\(n\) orbits with label
in \(\mathscr U\), and set
\begin{equation}\label{eq:harmonic-error-sequences}
 a_n:=p_{n,\mathscr U}\lambda^{-n}
      -\frac{\alpha_{\mathscr U}}{n},
 \qquad
 b_n:=p_{n,\mathscr U}\sum_{r\ge2}\frac{\lambda^{-rn}}{r}.
\end{equation}
The scalar M\"obius formula in the proof of \Cref{lem:class-mertens}, the
class-indicator expansion, and
\eqref{eq:explicit-primitive-trace-bound} imply, for every \(q_0<\xi<1\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:harmonic-error-bounds}
 a_n=O_\xi(\xi^n),\qquad b_n=O(\lambda^{-n}/n).
\end{equation}
The divisor factor in the M\"obius bounds is absorbed by the strict
inequality \(q_0<\xi\).

Taking real logarithms in \eqref{eq:union-class-product} gives
\[
 \log P_{\mathscr U}(N)
 =-\alpha_{\mathscr U}H_N-
   \sum_{n\le N}(a_n+b_n).
\]
On letting \(N\to\infty\) and comparing with the constant supplied by
\Cref{thm:correct-product}, one obtains
\(\log K_{\mathscr U}=-\alpha_{\mathscr U}\gamma-
\sum_{n\ge1}(a_n+b_n)\).  Hence the exact identity
\begin{equation}\label{eq:exact-harmonic-remainder}
 \log\frac{P_{\mathscr U}(N)N^{\alpha_{\mathscr U}}}{K_{\mathscr U}}
 =-\alpha_{\mathscr U}(H_N-\log N-\gamma)
  +\sum_{n>N}(a_n+b_n).
\end{equation}
The last sum is \(O_\xi(\xi^N)\) by
\eqref{eq:harmonic-error-bounds}; exponentiation proves
\eqref{eq:harmonic-renormalized-product}.  Finally, Euler--Maclaurin gives
\[
 H_N-\log N-\gamma
 \sim \frac{1}{2N}-\sum_{j\ge1}\frac{B_{2j}}{2jN^{2j}}.
\]
Substitution proves \eqref{eq:harmonic-logarithmic-jet}; exponentiating its
first terms gives \eqref{eq:harmonic-product-jet-three}.
\end{proof}

For Ihara prime cycles, Hasegawa and Saito obtained the scalar all-order
Bernoulli expansion for the second graph-theoretic Mertens sum
\cite{HasegawaSaito2016GraphMertens}.  In the aperiodic case their scalar
coefficients contain the \(\alpha_{\mathscr U}=1\) harmonic jet above, so no
priority is claimed here for those scalar Bernoulli coefficients.  The added
content of \Cref{thm:harmonic-renormalization} is the exact harmonic
renormalization, with exponential remainder, simultaneously for every
fixed-label union of Frobenius classes under the strict twisted gap.

\section{An exact \texorpdfstring{\(S_3\)}{S3} contradiction}
\label{sec:s3-witness}

We now give a strict-gap example in which the corrected and asserted Artin
coordinates are provably different.  The example is finite-dimensional; no
numerical spectral approximation enters the argument.

Let
\[
  A=\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\1&1\end{pmatrix},
  \qquad \lambda=2,
\]
and label the four directed edges by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:s3-labels}
 \begin{aligned}
  \tau(0\to0)&=(23),&\qquad \tau(0\to1)&=e,\\
  \tau(1\to0)&=(12),&\qquad \tau(1\to1)&=(123).
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Let \(\varepsilon\) and \(\varrho\) denote the sign and standard
representations of \(S_3\).

\begin{lemma}[Twisted determinants in the witness]\label{lem:s3-determinants}
For the cocycle \eqref{eq:s3-labels},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:s3-polynomials}
  P_{\mathbf1}(z)=1-2z,
  \qquad P_\varepsilon(z)=1,
  \qquad P_\varrho(z)=1+z.
\end{equation}
Consequently,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:s3-artin-values}
  L_\varepsilon(z)\equiv0,
  \qquad L_\varrho(z)=-\log(1+z),
\end{equation}
and the strict twisted gap holds with \(\eta=1<2\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
In the sign representation,
\[
  A_\varepsilon=
  \begin{pmatrix}-1&1\\-1&1\end{pmatrix},
  \qquad A_\varepsilon^2=0,
  \]
so \(P_\varepsilon(z)=1\).  In a non-orthonormal real model of the standard
representation, direct substitution of the four labels gives
\[
  A_\varrho=
  \begin{pmatrix}
    \frac12&\frac32&1&0\\
    \frac12&-\frac12&0&1\\
    -1&0&-\frac12&-\frac32\\
    0&1&\frac12&-\frac12
  \end{pmatrix}.
\]
Row reduction, or a direct determinant calculation, gives
\[
  \det(tI-A_\varrho)=t^3(t+1).
  \]
This rational real model is similar to a unitary realization: averaging any
positive definite inner product over \(S_3\) gives an invariant Gram matrix,
and conjugating by its positive square root makes the representation
orthogonal.  Similarity does not change the displayed determinant.  Thus
\(P_\varrho(z)=1+z\) and \(\rad(A_\varrho)=1\); the sign block has
spectral radius zero.  The logarithm identities follow from
\eqref{eq:artin-determinant-log}.
\end{proof}

The vanishing of \(L_\varepsilon\) does not imply the vanishing of the
primitive or fixed-label sign coordinates.  Indeed, the power map on the
quadratic character satisfies
\[
  \psi^m\varepsilon=
  \begin{cases}
    \varepsilon,&m\text{ odd},\\
    \mathbf1,&m\text{ even}.
  \end{cases}
\]

\begin{lemma}[Exact fixed-label sign coordinate]\label{lem:s3-fixed-sign}
For \(|z|<1/2\), with radial continuation to \(z=1/2\),
\begin{align}
  \Pi_\varepsilon(z)
   &=-\frac12\sum_{\substack{m\geq1\\m\text{ odd}}}
       \frac{\mu(m)}m\log(1-2z^{2m})^{-1},
       \label{eq:s3-pi-sign}\\
  F_\varepsilon(z)
   &=-\frac12\sum_{j\geq0}\frac1{2^j}
       \log\!\left(1-2z^{2^{j+1}}\right)^{-1}.
       \label{eq:s3-f-sign}
\end{align}
In particular,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:s3-strict-negative}
  F_\varepsilon(1/2)
  =-\frac12\sum_{j\geq0}\frac1{2^j}
       \log\!\left(1-2^{1-2^{j+1}}\right)^{-1}<0.
\end{equation}
The exact rational certificate described in \Cref{sec:exactness} sharpens
this to
\begin{equation}\label{eq:s3-rational-window}
  -\frac{381}{1000}<F_\varepsilon(1/2)<-\frac{380}{1000}.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Insert the two Adams cases above into
\eqref{eq:primitive-adams-mobius}.  The odd terms vanish because
\(L_\varepsilon=0\).  A squarefree even integer has the form \(2m\) with
\(m\) odd and \(\mu(2m)=-\mu(m)\), while
\(L_{\mathbf1}(z)=-\log(1-2z)\).  This gives
\eqref{eq:s3-pi-sign}.

Now insert \eqref{eq:s3-pi-sign} into
\(F_\varepsilon(z)=\sum_{q\geq1}\Pi_\varepsilon(z^q)/q\).  For a fixed
\(k=mq\), the coefficient multiplying
\(-\frac12\log(1-2z^{2k})^{-1}\) is
\[
  \frac1k\sum_{\substack{m\mid k\\m\text{ odd}}}\mu(m).
\]
This divisor sum is one precisely when the odd part of \(k\) is one, and is
zero otherwise.  Hence \(k=2^j\), proving \eqref{eq:s3-f-sign}.  The series
converges at \(z=1/2\), and every summand in
\eqref{eq:s3-strict-negative} is strictly negative.

It remains to prove the rational window without computational input.  Put
\[
 x_j:=2^{1-2^{j+1}},\qquad
 q_j:=(1-x_j)^{-1},\qquad
 u_j:=\frac{q_j-1}{q_j+1}=\frac{x_j}{2-x_j}.
\]
For \(j=0,1,2,3\), define the rational numbers
\begin{equation}\label{eq:s3-rational-log-brackets}
 L_j:=2\sum_{k=0}^{3}\frac{u_j^{2k+1}}{2k+1},
 \qquad
 U_j:=L_j+\frac{2u_j^9}{9(1-u_j^2)}.
\end{equation}
The identity
\(\log q=2\sum_{k\ge0}u^{2k+1}/(2k+1)\), for
\(u=(q-1)/(q+1)\in(0,1)\), and a geometric estimate of its positive tail give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:s3-rational-log-order}
 L_j<\log q_j<U_j\qquad(0\le j\le3).
\end{equation}
Moreover, \(\log(1-x)^{-1}<x/(1-x)\) and the double-exponential decrease of
\(x_j\) give the rational tail estimate
\begin{equation}\label{eq:s3-rational-tail}
 0<\frac12\sum_{j\ge4}2^{-j}\log q_j
   <\frac1{2^{35}-16}.
\end{equation}
Substitution of \(u_j=x_j/(2-x_j)\) in
\eqref{eq:s3-rational-log-brackets}, followed only by clearing positive
integer denominators, gives the exact rational comparisons
\begin{align}
 \frac{380}{1000}
 &<\frac12\sum_{j=0}^{3}2^{-j}L_j,
 \label{eq:s3-rational-lower-comparison}\\
 \frac12\sum_{j=0}^{3}2^{-j}U_j
   +\frac1{2^{35}-16}
 &<\frac{381}{1000}.
 \label{eq:s3-rational-upper-comparison}
\end{align}
Equations \eqref{eq:s3-strict-negative} and
\eqref{eq:s3-rational-log-order}--\eqref{eq:s3-rational-upper-comparison}
therefore imply
\[
 \frac{380}{1000}<-F_\varepsilon(1/2)<\frac{381}{1000},
\]
which is equivalent to \eqref{eq:s3-rational-window}.  The exact-arithmetic
program cited in \Cref{sec:exactness} independently checks the same rational
comparisons.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The \(S_3\) contradiction and its resolution]
\label{thm:s3-contradiction}
For the strict-gap extension \eqref{eq:s3-labels},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:s3-contradiction-coordinate}
  L_\varepsilon(1/2)=0,
  \qquad
  F_\varepsilon(1/2)\in
     \left(-\frac{381}{1000},-\frac{380}{1000}\right).
\end{equation}
Let \(C_t\) be the conjugacy class of transpositions, and let
\(K_{C_t}^{\mathrm{Art}}\) denote the value obtained from
\eqref{eq:correct-product-fourier} by replacing every \(F_\rho(1/2)\) with
\(L_\rho(1/2)\).  Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:s3-class-contradiction}
  \log\frac{K_{C_t}}{K_{C_t}^{\mathrm{Art}}}
  =\frac12F_\varepsilon(1/2)\ne 0.
\end{equation}
Thus the 1999 Artin-\(L\) constant and the exact primitive-class product
cannot both be correct.  The contradiction is resolved by the replacement
\(L_\rho\mapsto F_\rho\) in \Cref{thm:formal-correction}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The first assertion combines \Cref{lem:s3-determinants,lem:s3-fixed-sign}.
On the three classes \((\{e\},C_t,C_3)\), the non-trivial character rows are
\[
  \chi_\varepsilon=(1,-1,1),
  \qquad \chi_\varrho=(2,0,-1).
\]
Since \(|C_t|/|S_3|=1/2\), the standard coordinate vanishes from the
\(C_t\) Fourier component.  Subtracting the Artin-replaced logarithm from
\eqref{eq:correct-product-fourier} therefore gives
\[
  -\frac12\chi_\varepsilon(C_t)
    \bigl(F_\varepsilon(1/2)-L_\varepsilon(1/2)\bigr)
  =\frac12F_\varepsilon(1/2),
\]
which is non-zero by \eqref{eq:s3-contradiction-coordinate}.
\end{proof}

\section{Exact certificates and reproducibility}\label{sec:exactness}

\subsection{An exact strict-gap test}

Let \(\widetilde A\) be the non-negative integral adjacency matrix of the
regular skew product on \(V\times G\).  Its Peter--Weyl block decomposition
contains \(A_\rho\) with multiplicity \(\dim\rho\).  Thus the strict gap is
decidable from one integer characteristic polynomial, but the field in which
the Perron factor is removed must be stated correctly.

\begin{proposition}[Algebraic-number certificate for the strict gap]
\label{prop:exact-gap-certificate}
Let
\[
  \chi_{\widetilde A}(t)=\det(tI-\widetilde A)\in\ZZ[t],
  \qquad K=\QQ(\lambda).
\]
Division by the Perron factor is performed in \(K[t]\):
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gap-division-field}
  q(t):=\chi_{\widetilde A}(t)/(t-\lambda)\in K[t].
\end{equation}
Then \(\eta<\lambda\) if and only if every root \(\alpha\) of \(q\) satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:exact-root-comparison}
  \alpha\overline\alpha<\lambda^2.
\end{equation}
An exact certificate fixes the following compatible data:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=*]
  \item a squarefree polynomial \(f_\lambda\in\ZZ[t]\) and a rational
    isolating interval selecting \(\lambda\), thereby fixing the embedding
    \(K=\QQ(\lambda)\hookrightarrow\RR\);
  \item a tower from \(K\) to a splitting field \(L\) of \(q\), with defining
    polynomials and isolating rectangles for the successive generators, which
    fixes an embedding \(L\hookrightarrow\CC\) extending the chosen embedding
    of \(K\);
  \item a factorization of \(q\) in \(L[t]\), including multiplicities, and
    pairwise disjoint rational isolating rectangles for every embedded root
    \(\alpha_j\), together with an index \(j^*\) whose rectangle isolates
    \(\overline{\alpha_j}\);
  \item for every \(j\), the real algebraic number
    \[
      \beta_j:=\lambda^2-\alpha_j\overline{\alpha_j}\in L\cap\RR,
    \]
    a defining polynomial and a rational isolating interval contained in
    \((0,\infty)\);
  \item multiplicity data for \(\chi_{\widetilde A}\), its trivial block, and
    \(q\), verifying that exactly one simple trivial Perron root \(\lambda\)
    is removed and that every other root occurrence is represented.
\end{enumerate}
For a machine-readable record, integers are coefficient arrays, rationals are
reduced pairs \([p,q]\) with \(q>0\), real intervals are pairs of rationals,
and complex rectangles are pairs of real intervals.  Tower elements are
coefficient arrays in the declared power bases.  Each root record has six
named fields: \texttt{factor}, \texttt{multiplicity}, \texttt{rectangle},
\texttt{conjugate\_index}, \texttt{beta\_polynomial}, and
\texttt{beta\_interval}.
A verifier checks the tower relations and embeddings, the factorization
identity, disjoint root isolation, conjugate-rectangle pairing, the sum of
multiplicities, and positivity of every \texttt{beta\_interval}.  Sturm or
subresultant sign determination supplies the exact isolations.  Floating-point
root moduli are not certificate data.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The regular representation decomposes as
\(\bigoplus_{\rho\in\Irr(G)}(\dim\rho)\rho\).  Consequently the spectrum of
\(\widetilde A\), with algebraic multiplicity, is the union of the spectra of
the \(A_\rho\), each repeated \(\dim\rho\) times.  The trivial block is \(A\).
Primitivity makes \(\lambda\) a simple root and puts every other root of that
block strictly inside the Perron circle.  Removing this root in
\(\QQ(\lambda)[t]\) therefore leaves all non-trivial twisted roots together
with the harmless non-Perron base roots.  Condition
\eqref{eq:exact-root-comparison} for every remaining root is precisely
\(\max_{\rho\neq\mathbf1}\rad(A_\rho)<\lambda\).

The compatible embeddings make conjugation, multiplication, and sign
determination unambiguous.  Since each
\(\lambda^2-\alpha_j\overline{\alpha_j}\) is real algebraic, its isolating
interval decides its sign.  The multiplicity record prevents the removal of
any non-trivial occurrence of \(\lambda\).
\end{proof}

by

python certificates/s3\_log\_certificates.py
\end{center}
using exact rational arithmetic.  The script constructs the brackets in
\eqref{eq:s3-rational-log-brackets}, adds the explicit tails, and verifies the
stored primitive and fixed-label windows.  Its expected final line is
\texttt{fixed-label windows verified} (without terminal punctuation).  The program is
\texttt{certificates/s3\_log\_certificates.py} in the content-addressed public
repository record
\href{https://github.com/the-omega-institute/automath/tree/27d7e8fa74de6860723b81cd5cc3b01139909be7}{\texttt{27d7e8fa74de6860723b81cd5cc3b01139909be7}}.
Its SHA-256 digest is

\end{center}
The repository root is

strict-gap realization, and checks the squared normalization in
